send deployment command: ${(error as Error).message}`,\n      );\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Prepare a RUST_ENGINE program for a release bundle (enterprise plan\n   * Faz 5): everything `deployProgram` does EXCEPT the MQTT publish —\n   * the bundle command carries the artifact instead. Guards, atomic\n   * version increment, contract validation, content-addressed snapshot,\n   * deployment log (own correlation id) and the DEPLOYING status\n   * transition are identical, so bundle-borne and single-command deploys\n   * stay indistinguishable to every downstream consumer.\n   */\n  async prepareProgramBundleArtifact(\n    programId: string,\n    deviceId: string,\n    tenantId: string,\n    deployedBy: string,\n  ): Promise<ProgramBundleArtifact> {\n    if (!this.artifactService) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        'Artifact service not available — bundle deploys require the content-addressed store',\n      );\n    }\n\n    const program = await this.findByIdOrFail(programId, tenantId);\n    if (program.status !== ProgramStatus.APPROVED) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        `Program must be APPROVED before deployment. Current status: ${program.status}`,\n      );\n    }\n    if (program.deployTarget && program.deployTarget !== DeployTarget.RUST_ENGINE) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        `Program \"${program.programName}\" targets ${program.deployTarget} — only RUST_ENGINE programs can ride a release bundle`,\n      );\n    }\n\n    // Atomic version increment — same race guard as deployProgram.\n    await this.programRepo\n      .createQueryBuilder()\n      .update(AutomationProgram)\n      .set({ version: () => 'version + 1' })\n      .where('id = :id AND tenant_id = :tenantId', { id: programId, tenantId })\n      .execute();\n    const refreshedProgram = await this.findByIdOrFail(programId, tenantId);\n    program.version = refreshedProgram.version;\n\n    const edgeScript = await this.translateProgramToEdgeScript(program);\n    if (!validateDeployProgramParams(edgeScript)) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        `deploy_program payload violates the canonical contract: ${formatValidationErrors(validateDeployProgramParams)}`,\n      );\n    }\n\n    const artifact = await this.artifactService.snapshot(tenantId, {\n      artifactType: DeployArtifactType.AUTOMATION_PROGRAM,\n      content: edgeScript,\n      sourceEntityId: programId,\n      sourceEntityVersion: program.version,\n      createdBy: deployedBy,\n    });\n\n    const logCommandId = randomUUID();\n    if (this.deploymentLogService) {\n      await this.deploymentLogService.createLog({\n        tenantId,\n        programId,\n        deviceId,\n        commandId: logCommandId,\n        version: program.version,\n        edgeScript,\n        deployedBy,\n        artifactId: artifact.id,\n        checksumSha256: artifact.contentSha256,\n      });\n      await this.deploymentLogService.markDeploying(logCommandId);\n    }\n\n    await this.programRepo.update(program.id, {\n      status: ProgramStatus.DEPLOYING,\n      deployedVersion: program.version,\n      deployedAt: new Date(),\n      deployedBy: deployedBy,\n      deviceId: deviceId,\n    });\n\n    return {\n      programId,\n      version: program.version,\n      logCommandId,\n      artifactId: artifact.id,\n      sha256: artifact.contentSha256,\n    };\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Confirm deployment success (called when device reports back)\n   * v2.3: Transitions DEPLOYING → DEPLOYED on device confirmation\n   */\n  async confirmDeployment(\n    programId: string,\n    tenantId: string,\n    commandId: string,\n  ): Promise<AutomationProgram> {\n    const program = await this.findByIdOrFail(programId, tenantId);\n\n    if (program.status !== ProgramStatus.DEPLOYING) {\n      this.logger.warn(\n        `Deployment confirmation for program ${programId} in unexpected status: ${program.status}`,\n      );\n    }\n\n    program.status = ProgramStatus.DEPLOYED;\n    const saved = await this.programRepo.save(program);\n\n    // NOTE: deployment log is already updated by mqtt-listener before calling confirmDeployment\n\n    this.logger.log(`Program ${programId} deployment confirmed by device`);\n    return saved;\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Mark deployment as failed (called when device reports error or timeout)\n   * v2.3: Transitions DEPLOYING → APPROVED (back to deployable state)\n   */\n  async failDeployment(\n    programId: string,\n    tenantId: string,\n    commandId: string,\n    errorMessage: string,\n  ): Promise<AutomationProgram> {\n    const program = await this.findByIdOrFail(programId, tenantId);\n\n    if (program.status === ProgramStatus.DEPLOYING) {\n      program.status = ProgramStatus.APPROVED; // Revert to deployable\n    }\n\n    const saved = await this.programRepo.save(program);\n\n    // NOTE: deployment log is already updated by mqtt-listener before calling failDeployment\n\n    this.logger.error(`Program ${programId} deployment failed: ${errorMessage}`);\n    return saved;\n  }\n\n  // ==========================================================================\n  // SFC-to-Edge Translation\n  //","numLines":160,"startLine":1470,"totalLines":2824}}}

MANUAL = 'manual',           // Started/stopped manually\n  CONTINUOUS = 'continuous',   // Auto-start on device boot\n  SCHEDULED = 'scheduled',     // Runs on schedule\n  TRIGGERED = 'triggered',     // Runs when condition met\n}\n\nregisterEnumType(ExecutionMode, {\n  name: 'ExecutionMode',\n  description: 'How the program is triggered to run',\n});\n\n/**\n * Program status\n */\nexport enum ProgramStatus {\n  DRAFT = 'draft',             // Being edited\n  PENDING_REVIEW = 'pending_review', // Awaiting approval\n  APPROVED = 'approved',       // Ready to deploy\n  DEPLOYING = SharedMqttModule,\n406-\n407-    // Feature modules\n408-    SensorModule,\n409-    HealthModule,\n410-\n411-    /**\n412:     * SECURITY (HIGH-004): Tenant Row-Level Security (schema-per-tenant).\n413:     * sensor-service data lives in per-tenant schemas created via\n414-     * CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL, which does NOT copy RLS policies.\n415:     * syncTenantSchemas makes RlsModule iterate every tenant_<uuid> schema\n416-     * at OnApplicationBootstrap and install the canonical policy on each.\n417-     */\n418-    RlsModule.forPoolService({\n419-      serviceName: 'sensor',\n420-      // PR#363 port: runtime per-tenant RLS sweep only when db-migrate is\n421-      // NOT authoritative — production tenants get the same policies from\n422-      // the db-migrate tenant fan-out hardening.\n423-      syncTenantSchemas: !sensorSchemaDdlOwnedByDbMigrate,\n--\n477-    SensorTypeModule,\n478-\n479-    // Device group management and batch operations\n480-    DeviceGroupModule,\n481-\n482-    // Real-time stream processing (anomaly detection, rate-of-change, Kafka)\n483-    StreamProcessingModule,\n484-\n485: schema-existence cache + TenantProvisioned invalidation\n491-    // (no stale-negative-cache block for freshly provisioned tenants).\n492-    TenantSchemaCacheModule,\n493-    SchemaDriftModule.forRoot({ serviceName: 'sensor' }),\n494-  ],\n495-  providers: [\n496-    SensorSchemaVersionGate,\n497-    // Global exception filter\n498-    {\n--\n527-    // global registration is safe and makes every @RequireTenantPermission\n528-    // (e.g. 'edge:manage-io-config') self-enforcing instead of dead metadata.\n529-    {\n530-      provide: APP_GUARD,\n531-      useFactory: (reflector: Reflector): TenantPermissionGuard =>\n532-        new TenantPermissionGuard(reflector),\n533-      inject: [Reflector],\n534-    },\n535:    // Bootstrap source schema tables on startup (creates template tables if missing)\n536-    SourceSchemaBootstrapService,\n537:    // Pool-level tenant schema routing (patches pg Pool.connect for search_path injection)\n538-    if (!pkg) throw new NotFoundException(`ScadaPackage ${packageId} not found`);\n    this.assertPackageDeployable(pkg);\n\n    // Determine which programs ride the bundle\n    const meta = pkg.packageData.meta as Record<string, unknown> | undefined;\n    const bindings: AutomationBinding[] | undefined = meta?.automationBindings as AutomationBinding[] | undefined;\n    const programIds = programIdOverrides && programIdOverrides.length > 0\n      ? programIdOverrides\n      : [...new Set((bindings || []).map((b) => b.programId).filter(Boolean))];\n\n    // Bundle pipeline preconditions — fail loudly with the exact remedy.\n    if (!this.edgeDeviceService) {\n      throw new BadRequestException('Edge device service not available');\n    }\n    if (!this.artifactService) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        'Artifact service not available — bundle deploys require the content-addressed store',\n      );\n    }\n    const releaseBundleService = this.releaseBundleService;\n    const outboxPublisher = this.outboxPublisher;\n    if (!releaseBundleService || !outboxPublisher) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        'Release-bundle pipeline not available (ReleaseBundleModule / outbox not wired)',\n      );\n    }\n    const deploySigningService = this.deploySigningService;\n    if (!deploySigningService?.isConfigured) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        'Bundle deploys require deploy signing — set SENSOR_DEPLOY_SIGNING_KEY_SEED_HEX (unsigned bundles do not exist)',\n      );\n    }\n    const automationService = this.automationService;\n    if (programIds.length > 0 && !automationService) {\n      throw new BadRequestException(\n        'AutomationService not available — cannot bundle automation programs',\n      );\n    }\n\n    const device = await this.edgeDeviceService.findByIdOrFail(deviceId, tenantId);\n    if (!device.isOnline) {\n      return {\n        success: false,\n        message: 'Device is offline — cannot deploy release bundle',\n        automationResults: [],\n      };\n    }\n\n    const automationResults: AutomationDeployStepResult[] = [];\n    const artifactRefs: ReleaseBundleArtifactRef[] = [];\n\n    // Step 1: stage automation programs into the bundle (programs lead so\n    // they are running before SCADA reads their variables — same ordering\n    // contract as before, now enforced by manifest order + edge apply).\n    for (const programId of programIds) {\n      if (!automationService) break;\n      try {\n        const prep = await automationService.prepareProgramBundleArtifact(\n          programId,\n          deviceId,\n          tenantId,\n          userId || 'system',\n        );\n        artifactRefs.push({\n          artifactId: prep.artifactId,\n          kind: DeployArtifactType.AUTOMATION_PROGRAM,\n          sha256: prep.sha256,\n          sourceEntityId: programId,\n          logCommandId: prep.logCommandId,\n          version: prep.version,\n        });\n        automationResults.push({\n          programId,\n          success: true,\n          message: 'Staged into release bundle',\n          commandId: prep.logCommandId,\n        });\n      } catch (error) {\n        const errMsg = (error as Error).message;\n        automationResults.push({ programId, success: false, message: errMsg });\n        return {\n          success: false,\n          message: `Automation program ${programId} staging failed: ${errMsg}. Bundle aborted — nothing published.`,\n          automationResults,\n        };\n      }\n    }\n\n    // Step 2: stage the SCADA package artifact\n    let packageRef: ReleaseBundleArtifactRef;\n    try {\n      packageRef = await this.prepareScadaPackageBundleArtifact(pkg, device, tenantId, userId);\n      artifactRefs.push(packageRef);\n    } catch (error) {\n      const errMsg = (error as Error).message;\n      return {\n        success: false,\n        message: `SCADA package staging failed: ${errMsg}. Bundle aborted — nothing published.`,\n        automationResults,\n        scadaResult: { packageId, success: false, message: errMsg },\n      };\n    }\n\n    // Step 3: signed manifest + transactional PENDING row + outbox event\n    const bundleId = randomUUID();\n    const commandId = randomUUID();\n    const previous = await releaseBundleService.findLastConfirmed(tenantId, device.id);\n    const manifest: ReleaseBundleManifest = { bundleId, artifacts: artifactRefs };\n    const manifestSha256 = createHash('sha256')\n      .update(canonicalJsonStringify(manifest))","numLines":110,"startLine":1380,"totalLines":1679}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NEFcVc793oMe6maGbgb7MC","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { DynamicModule, Module, Provider, Type } from '@nestjs/common';\n2\timport { DataSource } from 'typeorm';\n3\timport { BypassRlsService } from './bypass-rls.service';\n4\timport { createRlsConnectionBootstrap } from './rls-connection-bootstrap.service';\n5\timport {\n6\t  RlsSchemaBootstrap,\n7\t  RlsSchemaBootstrapOptions,\n8\t} from './rls-schema-bootstrap.service';\n9\timport {\n10\t  TenantRlsSyncService,\n11\t  TenantRlsSyncOptions,\n12\t} from './tenant-rls-sync.service';\n13\t\n14\t/**\n15\t * RlsModule\n16\t * ============================================================================\n17\t *\n18\t * Tier-1 typed API for tenant Row-Level Security wiring.\n19\t *\n20\t * # Why there are two entry points (and not one `forRoot`)\n21\t *\n22\t * The legacy `forRoot({ serviceName })` signature had a subtle footgun: it\n23\t * unconditionally registered `RlsConnectionBootstrap`, which constructor-\n24\t * injects `DataSource`. A service without `TypeOrmModule.forRoot(...)` in\n25\t * its imports graph (e.g., gateway-api) would crash at DI resolution time\n26\t * with a cryptic `Nest can't resolve dependencies of RlsConnectionBootstrapImpl`\n27\t * deep in the bootstrap path — exactly what the 2026-04-14 gateway-api\n28\t * outage was (fixed site-locally in `607f9d9d`).\n29\t *\n30\t * Root-cause fix per CLAUDE.md architectural hierarchy:\n31\t *\n32\t *   - **Tier-1 at the API surface**: two distinct named static methods —\n33\t *     `forPoolService(...)` and `forBypassOnly(...)` — make the decision\n34\t *     structural. The service author chooses the pool-bound variant OR the\n35\t *     bypass-only variant at import time; there is no code path that\n36\t *     registers pool-bound providers without declaring a pool dependency.\n37\t *\n38\t *   - **Tier-3 runtime guard**: inside `forPoolService`, the pool bootstrap\n39\t *     asserts the `DataSource` it receives is a usable pg pool and throws\n40\t *     a LOUD, actionable error with remediation steps if the token was\n41\t *     resolved from a non-pool source (or is missing a pg master). This\n42\t *     catches edge cases NestJS DI cannot statically prove — e.g., a\n43\t *     misconfigured `TypeOrmModule` that resolves `DataSource` to a stub.\n44\t *\n45\t * The two mechanisms compose: the compile-time API split prevents the\n46\t * common case; the runtime guard catches configuration-level drift.\n47\t *\n48\t * # `forPoolService` — services that OWN a TypeORM DataSource\n49\t *\n50\t * Requires `TypeOrmModule.forRoot(...)` (or `forRootAsync(...)`) in the\n51\t * host module's imports. Registers:\n52\t *\n53\t *   1. `RlsConnectionBootstrap` — pool patch that injects\n54\t *      `app.current_tenant` + `app.bypass_rls` GUCs on every checkout,\n55\t *      sourced from `AsyncLocalStorage`. Pairs with the\n56\t *      `tenant_isolation_policy` installed by `applyTenantRlsToSchema`.\n57\t *\n58\t *   2. `BypassRlsService` — scoped, audited bypass for SUPER_ADMIN\n59\t *      endpoints and background workers.\n60\t *\n61\t *   3. **(optional)** `RlsSchemaBootstrap` when `autoApply: true` — runs\n62\t *      `applyTenantRlsToSchema` at `OnApplicationBootstrap`. Used by\n63\t *      services WITHOUT a TypeORM migration runner (billing, ai,\n64\t *      notification, alert, config, event-store). Services WITH a\n65\t *      migration runner (farm, sensor, hr, messaging) install policies\n66\t *      via a regular migration and should leave `autoApply` at its\n67\t *      default (`false`).\n68\t *\n69\t *   4. **(optional)** `TenantRlsSyncService` when `syncTenantSchemas:\n70\t *      true` — iterates every `tenant_<uuid>` schema at\n71\t *      `OnApplicationBootstrap` and reinstalls the canonical\n72\t *      `tenant_isolation_policy`. Required for schema-per-tenant\n73\t *      services because `CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL` does NOT\n74\t *      propagate RLS policies to tenant-cloned tables.\n75\t *\n76\t * ```ts\n77\t * import { RlsModule } from '@aquaculture/backend-common';\n78\t *\n79\t * @Module({\n80\t *   imports: [\n81\t *     TypeOrmModule.forRoot({ ... }),\n82\t *     RlsModule.forPoolService({\n83\t *       serviceName: 'billing',\n84\t *       autoApply: true,\n85\t *       excludeTables: ['billing_outbox'],\n86\t *     }),\n87\t *   ],\n88\t * })\n89\t * export class AppModule {}\n90\t * ```\n91\t *\n92\t * # `forBypassOnly` — services that need `BypassRlsService` but have NO pool\n93\t *\n94\t * For services that must use `BypassRlsService` (background workers,\n95\t * impersonation auditors that call remote subgraphs, etc.) but do NOT own\n96\t * a local TypeORM pool. Registers ONLY `BypassRlsService`.\n97\t *\n98\t * NOTE: Pool-less services that do NOT need bypass should not import\n99\t * `RlsModule` at all. Gateway-api is the canonical example — it has no\n100\t * DataSource and no rows to bypass, so it stays out of the RLS module\n101\t * graph entirely (the commit history of `607f9d9d` and the comment\n102\t * block in gateway-api's `app.module.ts` serve as the architectural\n103\t * reference for this negative case).\n104\t *\n105\t * ```ts\n106\t * import { RlsModule } from '@aquaculture/backend-common';\n107\t *\n108\t * @Module({\n109\t *   imports: [\n110\t *     RlsModule.forBypassOnly({ serviceName: 'some-bypass-only-service' }),\n111\t *   ],\n112\t * })\n113\t * export class AppModule {}\n114\t * ```\n115\t *\n116\t * # Registration is explicit; consumption is global-within-service\n117\t *\n118\t * Each service MUST call one of the static methods in its AppModule —\n119\t * there is no auto-registration. This guarantees \"I forgot to wire RLS\n120\t * into the new service\" is caught at service setup time.\n121\t *\n122\t * Once registered, the export (`BypassRlsService` in both the pool and\n123\t * bypass-only variants) is `global: true` within that service's module tree, so\n124\t * feature modules can inject them without re-importing `RlsModule`.\n125\t * This is deliberate: these services have no per-service configuration\n126\t * (audit label is caller-supplied) and requiring every submodule that\n127\t * needs bypass to thread `RlsModule` through its imports creates\n128\t * import-order hazards with no architectural payoff.\n129\t */\n130\t\n131\t/**\n132\t * Options accepted by `RlsModule.forPoolService()`.\n133\t *\n134\t * Combines wiring concerns (`serviceName`) with the helper's options\n135\t * (`excludeTables`, `tenantIdColumns`) and the two optional bootstraps\n136\t * (`autoApply`, `syncTenantSchemas`).\n137\t */\n138\texport interface RlsPoolServiceOptions {\n139\t  /**\n140\t   * Lowercase service tag used in log prefixes.\n141\t   * Must match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*$`.\n142\t   * Example: `'billing'`, `'ai-service'`, `'notification'`.\n143\t   */\n144\t  serviceName: string;\n145\t  /**\n146\t   * If `true`, register `RlsSchemaBootstrap` so the helper runs at\n147\t   * `OnApplicationBootstrap` and installs policies on every cold start.\n148\t   * Use this for services that have NO TypeORM migration runner.\n149\t   *\n150\t   * Defaults to `false` — services with their own migration runner should\n151\t   * call `applyTenantRlsToSchema` from a regular migration instead, so the\n152\t   * deploy pipeline keeps a single source of truth for schema state.\n153\t   */\n154\t  autoApply?: boolean;\n155\t  /**\n156\t   * Tables to skip when discovering tenant-scoped tables. Forwarded into\n157\t   * `applyTenantRlsToSchema` and ignored when `autoApply` is false.\n158\t   * @see ApplyTenantRlsOptions.excludeTables\n159\t   */\n160\t  excludeTables?: readonly string[];\n161\t  /**\n162\t   * Override the discovered tenant column names. Defaults to\n163\t   * `['tenantId', 'tenant_id']`. Forwarded into `applyTenantRlsToSchema`\n164\t   * and ignored when `autoApply` is false.\n165\t   * @see ApplyTenantRlsOptions.tenantIdColumns\n166\t   */\n167\t  tenantIdColumns?: readonly string[];\n168\t  /**\n169\t   * If `true`, register `TenantRlsSyncService` so the helper iterates\n170\t   * every `tenant_<uuid>` schema at `OnApplicationBootstrap` and"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0758df33-8c42-48c2-9267-90bb24a8128c","uuid":"c3a59015-2124-412f-bb09-8a721a550a4f","timestamp":"2026-10-08T09:41:55.827Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-e6911ae6722d/libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/rls.module.ts","content":"import { DynamicModule, Module, Provider, Type } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { DataSource } from 'typeorm';\nimport { BypassRlsService } from './bypass-rls.service';\nimport { createRlsConnectionBootstrap } from './rls-connection-bootstrap.service';\nimport {\n  RlsSchemaBootstrap,\n  RlsSchemaBootstrapOptions,\n} from './rls-schema-bootstrap.service';\nimport {\n  TenantRlsSyncService,\n  TenantRlsSyncOptions,\n} from './tenant-rls-sync.service';\n\n/**\n * RlsModule\n * ============================================================================\n *\n * Tier-1 typed API for tenant Row-Level Security wiring.\n *\n * # Why there are two entry points (and not one `forRoot`)\n *\n * The legacy `forRoot({ serviceName })` signature had a subtle footgun: it\n * unconditionally registered `RlsConnectionBootstrap`, which constructor-\n * injects `DataSource`. A service without `TypeOrmModule.forRoot(...)` in\n * its imports graph (e.g., gateway-api) would crash at DI resolution time\n * with a cryptic `Nest can't resolve dependencies of RlsConnectionBootstrapImpl`\n * deep in the bootstrap path — exactly what the 2026-04-14 gateway-api\n * outage was (fixed site-locally in `607f9d9d`).\n *\n * Root-cause fix per CLAUDE.md architectural hierarchy:\n *\n *   - **Tier-1 at the API surface**: two distinct named static methods —\n *     `forPoolService(...)` and `forBypassOnly(...)` — make the decision\n *     structural. The service author chooses the pool-bound variant OR the\n *     bypass-only variant at import time; there is no code path that\n *     registers pool-bound providers without declaring a pool dependency.\n *\n *   - **Tier-3 runtime guard**: inside `forPoolService`, the pool bootstrap\n *     asserts the `DataSource` it receives is a usable pg pool and throws\n *     a LOUD, actionable error with remediation steps if the token was\n *     resolved from a non-pool source (or is missing a pg master). This\n *     catches edge cases NestJS DI cannot statically prove — e.g., a\n *     misconfigured `TypeOrmModule` that resolves `DataSource` to a stub.\n *\n * The two mechanisms compose: the compile-time API split prevents the\n * common case; the runtime guard catches configuration-level drift.\n *\n * # `forPoolService` — services that OWN a TypeORM DataSource\n *\n * Requires `TypeOrmModule.forRoot(...)` (or `forRootAsync(...)`) in the\n * host module's imports. Registers:\n *\n *   1. `RlsConnectionBootstrap` — pool patch that injects\n *      `app.current_tenant` + `app.bypass_rls` GUCs on every checkout,\n *      sourced from `AsyncLocalStorage`. Pairs with the\n *      `tenant_isolation_policy` installed by `applyTenantRlsToSchema`.\n *\n *   2. `BypassRlsService` — scoped, audited bypass for SUPER_ADMIN\n *      endpoints and background workers.\n *\n *   3. **(optional)** `RlsSchemaBootstrap` when `autoApply: true` — runs\n *      `applyTenantRlsToSchema` at `OnApplicationBootstrap`. Used by\n *      services WITHOUT a TypeORM migration runner (billing, ai,\n *      notification, alert, config, event-store). Services WITH a\n *      migration runner (farm, sensor, hr, messaging) install policies\n *      via a regular migration and should leave `autoApply` at its\n *      default (`false`).\n *\n *   4. **(optional)** `TenantRlsSyncService` when `syncTenantSchemas:\n *      true` — iterates every `tenant_<uuid>` schema at\n *      `OnApplicationBootstrap` and reinstalls the canonical\n *      `tenant_isolation_policy`. Required for schema-per-tenant\n *      services because `CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL` does NOT\n *      propagate RLS policies to tenant-cloned tables.\n *\n * ```ts\n * import { RlsModule } from '@aquaculture/backend-common';\n *\n * @Module({\n *   imports: [\n *     TypeOrmModule.forRoot({ ... }),\n *     RlsModule.forPoolService({\n *       serviceName: 'billing',\n *       autoApply: true,\n *       excludeTables: ['billing_outbox'],\n *     }),\n *   ],\n * })\n * export class AppModule {}\n * ```\n *\n * # `forBypassOnly` — services that need `BypassRlsService` but have NO pool\n *\n * For services that must use `BypassRlsService` (background workers,\n * impersonation auditors that call remote subgraphs, etc.) but do NOT own\n * a local TypeORM pool. Registers ONLY `BypassRlsService`.\n *\n * NOTE: Pool-less services that do NOT need bypass should not import\n * `RlsModule` at all. Gateway-api is the canonical example — it has no\n * DataSource and no rows to bypass, so it stays out of the RLS module\n * graph entirely (the commit history of `607f9d9d` and the comment\n * block in gateway-api's `app.module.ts` serve as the architectural\n * reference for this negative case).\n *\n * ```ts\n * import { RlsModule } from '@aquaculture/backend-common';\n *\n * @Module({\n *   imports: [\n *     RlsModule.forBypassOnly({ serviceName: 'some-bypass-only-service' }),\n *   ],\n * })\n * export class AppModule {}\n * ```\n *\n * # Registration is explicit; consumption is global-within-service\n *\n * Each service MUST call one of the static methods in its AppModule —\n * there is no auto-registration. This guarantees \"I forgot to wire RLS\n * into the new service\" is caught at service setup time.\n *\n * Once registered, the export (`BypassRlsService` in both the pool and\n * bypass-only variants) is `global: true` within that service's module tree, so\n * feature modules can inject them without re-importing `RlsModule`.\n * This is deliberate: these services have no per-service configuration\n * (audit label is caller-supplied) and requiring every submodule that\n * needs bypass to thread `RlsModule` through its imports creates\n * import-order hazards with no architectural payoff.\n */\n\n/**\n * Options accepted by `RlsModule.forPoolService()`.\n *\n * Combines wiring concerns (`serviceName`) with the helper's options\n * (`excludeTables`, `tenantIdColumns`) and the two optional bootstraps\n * (`autoApply`, `syncTenantSchemas`).\n */\nexport interface RlsPoolServiceOptions {\n  /**\n   * Lowercase service tag used in log prefixes.\n   * Must match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*$`.\n   * Example: `'billing'`, `'ai-service'`, `'notification'`.\n   */\n  serviceName: string;\n  /**\n   * If `true`, register `RlsSchemaBootstrap` so the helper runs at\n   * `OnApplicationBootstrap` and installs policies on every cold start.\n   * Use this for services that have NO TypeORM migration runner.\n   *\n   * Defaults to `false` — services with their own migration runner should\n   * call `applyTenantRlsToSchema` from a regular migration instead, so the\n   * deploy pipeline keeps a single source of truth for schema state.\n   */\n  autoApply?: boolean;\n  /**\n   * Tables to skip when discovering tenant-scoped tables. Forwarded into\n   * `applyTenantRlsToSchema` and ignored when `autoApply` is false.\n   * @see ApplyTenantRlsOptions.excludeTables\n   */\n  excludeTables?: readonly string[];\n  /**\n   * Override the discovered tenant column names. Defaults to\n   * `['tenantId', 'tenant_id']`. Forwarded into `applyTenantRlsToSchema`\n   * and ignored when `autoApply` is false.\n   * @see ApplyTenantRlsOptions.tenantIdColumns\n   */\n  tenantIdColumns?: readonly string[];\n  /**\n   * If `true`, register `TenantRlsSyncService` so the helper iterates\n   * every `tenant_<uuid>` schema at `OnApplicationBootstrap` and","numLines":170,"startLine":1,"totalLines":318}}}

       await ddl.manager.save(\n          ddl.manager.create(DeploymentLog, {\n            tenantId,\n            programId: program.id,\n            deviceId: randomUUID(),\n            commandId: randomUUID(),\n            version: 1,\n            status: DeploymentStatus.DEPLOYING,\n            deployedAt: AN_HOUR_AGO,\n          }),\n        );\n      },\n    });\n    admin = stage.admin;\n    runtime = stage.runtime;\n  });\n\n  afterAll(async () => {\n    await stage?.shutdown();\n  });\n\n  const scheduledJobs = createScheduledJobTestExecutor().executor;\n  function unusedRepository<T extends object>(): Repository<T> {\n    return {} as Partial<Repository<T>> as Repository<T>;\n  }\n\n  it('marks a silent edge device offline in every tenant (was: zero rows)', async () => {\n    const service = new EdgeDeviceService(\n      unusedRepository<EdgeDevice>(),\n      unusedRepository<DeviceIoConfig>(),\n      unusedRepository<LoRaDevice>(),\n      runtime!,\n      scheduledJobs,\n      null,\n      {} as Partial<InstallerScriptService> as InstallerScriptService,\n      { get: () => undefined } as Partial<ConfigService> as ConfigService,\n    );\n\n    await expect(service.markStaleDevicesOffline(5)).resolves.toBe(2);\n\n    for (const tenantId of [TENANT_A, TENANT_B]) {\n      const [device] = await admin!.query(\n        `SELECT is_online, lifecycle_state FROM \"${schemas[tenantId]}\".edge_devices`,\n      );\n      expect(device).toEqual({ is_online: false, lifecycle_state: 'offline' });\n    }\n  });\n\n  it('reverts a deploy stuck past the timeout and fails its log (was: never reverted)', async () => {\n    const service = new AutomationService(\n      unusedRepository(),\n      unusedRepository(),\n      unusedRepository(),\n      unusedRepository(),\n      unusedRepository(),\n      runtime!,\n      scheduledJobs,\n      {} as Partial<EdgeDeviceService> as EdgeDeviceService,\n      {} as Partial<MqttClientService> as MqttClientService,\n      {} as Partial<DeploymentLogService> as DeploymentLogService,\n      {} as Partial<AutomationEventsPublisher> as AutomationEventsPublisher,\n      {} as Partial<ArtifactService> as ArtifactService,\n    );\n\n    await service.checkDeployTimeout();\n\n    for (const tenantId of [TENANT_A, TENANT_B]) {\n      const schema = schemas[tenantId];\n      const [program] = await admin!.query(\n        `SELECT status FROM \"${schema}\".automation_programs WHERE id = $1`,\n        [programIds[tenantId]],\n      );\n      const [log] = await admin!.query(\n        `SELECT status FROM \"${schema}\".deployment_logs WHERE program_id = $1`,\n        [programIds[tenantId]],\n      );\n      expect(program.status).toBe('approved');\n      expect(log.status).toBe('failed');\n    }\n  });\n});\n","numLines":82,"startLine":80,"totalLines":161}}}

The pool IS created with `options: '-c search_path=<src>,public'`\n46: * mutable: anything that runs `SET search_path = public` on a pooled\n62: * the pool MUST have its search_path re-asserted before the caller receives\n92:      // which let a schema-per-tenant service boot with search_path routing\n94:      // search_path instead of tenant_<uuid>, which is the failure this\n103:      const defaultSearchPath = `SET search_path TO \"${src}\", public`;\n132:                `SET search_path TO \"${schemaName}\", \"${src}\", public`,\n135:                    logger.error(`Failed to set search_path to ${schemaName}: ${qErr.message}`);\n151:                  logger.error(`Failed to set default search_path (${src},public): ${qErr.message}`);\n176:              await client.query(`SET search_path TO \"${schemaName}\", \"${src}\", public`);\n178:              logger.error(`Failed to set search_path to ${schemaName}: ${(qErr as Error).message}`);\n189:              logger.error(`Failed to set default search_path (${src},public): ${(qErr as Error).message}`);\n199:        `PostgreSQL connection pool patched for tenant-aware search_path routing ` +"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0758df33-8c42-48c2-9267-90bb24a8128c","uuid":"943adfb8-2c53-4759-9c5c-fa695ced4065","timestamp":"2026-10-08T09:42:37.952Z","tool_use_result":{"mode":"content","numFiles":0,"filenames":[],"content":"22: * EVERY connection checkout has a deterministic, known-correct `search_path`:\n24: *   - When a tenant context is present in AsyncLocalStorage, the search_path\n28: *     `schemaName` or the canonical `tenantId` in AsyncLocalStorage; this keeps\n34: *     seed services, cron jobs), the search_path becomes\n38: * # Why the non-request branch MUST also set search_path (root cause of\n41: * An earlier revision of this patch only set the search_path in the tenant\n43: * checkouts. The pool IS created with `options: '-c search_path=<src>,public'`\n46: * mutable: anything that runs `SET search_path = public` on a pooled\n62: * the pool MUST have its search_path re-asserted before the caller receives\n92:      // which let a schema-per-tenant service boot with search_path routing\n94:      // search_path instead of tenant_<uuid>, which is the failure this\n103:      const defaultSearchPath = `SET search_path TO \"${src}\", public`;\n132:                `SET search_path TO \"${schemaName}\", \"${src}\", public`,\n135:                    logger.error(`Failed to set search_path to ${schemaName}: ${qErr.message}`);\n151:                  logger.error(`Failed to set default search_path (${src},public): ${qErr.message}`);\n176:              await client.query(`SET search_path TO \"${schemaName}\", \"${src}\", public`);\n178:              logger.error(`Failed to set search_path to ${schemaName}: ${(qErr as Error).message}`);\n189:              logger.error(`Failed to set default search_path (${src},public): ${(qErr as Error).message}`);\n199:        `PostgreSQL connection pool patched for tenant-aware search_path routing ` +","numLines":19,"totalLines":19}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HqJfGQ6GSbyFzDvH72VbEr","type":"tool_result","content":"420\t * leaves a window in which the owner reads nothing.\n421\t */\n422\tasync function reconcileContinuousAggregateOwnerPolicy(\n423\t  qr: QueryRunner,\n424\t  schema: string,\n425\t  tableName: string,\n426\t  owners: readonly string[],\n427\t  hasPolicy: boolean,\n428\t  logger: RlsHelperLogger,\n429\t): Promise<void> {\n430\t  const policy = CONTINUOUS_AGGREGATE_OWNER_READ_POLICY_NAME;\n431\t  if (owners.length === 0) {\n432\t    if (hasPolicy) {\n433\t      await qr.query(`DROP POLICY IF EXISTS \"${policy}\" ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"`);\n434\t      logger.log(`Owner read policy dropped on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" (no aggregate left)`);\n435\t    }\n436\t    return;\n437\t  }\n438\t  for (const owner of owners) assertSafeIdentifier(owner, 'continuous aggregate owner');\n439\t\n440\t  const roleArray = `ARRAY[${owners.map((owner) => `'${owner}'`).join(', ')}]::name[]`;\n441\t  const roleList = owners.map((owner) => `\"${owner}\"`).join(', ');\n442\t  await qr.query(`\n443\t    DO $owner_read$\n444\t    DECLARE\n445\t      current_policy record;\n446\t    BEGIN\n447\t      SELECT p.cmd, p.qual, p.permissive, p.roles INTO current_policy\n448\t        FROM pg_policies p\n449\t       WHERE p.schemaname = '${schema}'\n450\t         AND p.tablename = '${tableName}'\n451\t         AND p.policyname = '${policy}';\n452\t      IF FOUND\n453\t         AND current_policy.cmd = 'SELECT'\n454\t         AND current_policy.qual = 'true'\n455\t         AND current_policy.permissive = 'PERMISSIVE'\n456\t         AND current_policy.roles @> ${roleArray}\n457\t         AND current_policy.roles <@ ${roleArray} THEN\n458\t        RETURN;\n459\t      END IF;\n460\t      IF FOUND THEN\n461\t        DROP POLICY \"${policy}\" ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\";\n462\t      END IF;\n463\t      CREATE POLICY \"${policy}\" ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"\n464\t        AS PERMISSIVE FOR SELECT TO ${roleList} USING (true);\n465\t    END\n466\t    $owner_read$`);\n467\t  logger.log(`Owner read policy ensured on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" for ${owners.join(', ')}`);\n468\t}\n469\t\n470\tasync function discoverTimescaleTableState(\n471\t  qr: QueryRunner,\n472\t  schema: string,\n473\t  logger: RlsHelperLogger,\n474\t): Promise<TimescaleTableState> {\n475\t  assertSafeIdentifier(schema, 'schema');\n476\t\n477\t  const metadataColumns = await queryRows<{ column_name: string }>(\n478\t    qr,\n479\t    `\n480\t      SELECT column_name\n481\t      FROM information_schema.columns\n482\t      WHERE table_schema = 'timescaledb_information'\n483\t        AND table_name = 'hypertables'\n484\t        AND column_name = ANY($1::text[])\n485\t    `,\n486\t    [['columnstore_enabled', 'compression_enabled']],\n487\t  );\n488\t\n489\t  const supportedColumns = new Set(metadataColumns.map((row) => row.column_name));"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0758df33-8c42-48c2-9267-90bb24a8128c","uuid":"1c634c39-d2d1-42d1-8de4-fbf7e21bd412","timestamp":"2026-10-08T09:42:54.573Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-e6911ae6722d/libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts","content":" * leaves a window in which the owner reads nothing.\n */\nasync function reconcileContinuousAggregateOwnerPolicy(\n  qr: QueryRunner,\n  schema: string,\n  tableName: string,\n  owners: readonly string[],\n  hasPolicy: boolean,\n  logger: RlsHelperLogger,\n): Promise<void> {\n  const policy = CONTINUOUS_AGGREGATE_OWNER_READ_POLICY_NAME;\n  if (owners.length === 0) {\n    if (hasPolicy) {\n      await qr.query(`DROP POLICY IF EXISTS \"${policy}\" ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"`);\n      logger.log(`Owner read policy dropped on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" (no aggregate left)`);\n    }\n    return;\n  }\n  for (const owner of owners) assertSafeIdentifier(owner, 'continuous aggregate owner');\n\n  const roleArray = `ARRAY[${owners.map((owner) => `'${owner}'`).join(', ')}]::name[]`;\n  const roleList = owners.map((owner) => `\"${owner}\"`).join(', ');\n  await qr.query(`\n    DO $owner_read$\n    DECLARE\n      current_policy record;\n    BEGIN\n      SELECT p.cmd, p.qual, p.permissive, p.roles INTO current_policy\n        FROM pg_policies p\n       WHERE p.schemaname = '${schema}'\n         AND p.tablename = '${tableName}'\n         AND p.policyname = '${policy}';\n      IF FOUND\n         AND current_policy.cmd = 'SELECT'\n         AND current_policy.qual = 'true'\n         AND current_policy.permissive = 'PERMISSIVE'\n         AND current_policy.roles @> ${roleArray}\n         AND current_policy.roles <@ ${roleArray} THEN\n        RETURN;\n      END IF;\n      IF FOUND THEN\n        DROP POLICY \"${policy}\" ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\";\n      END IF;\n      CREATE POLICY \"${policy}\" ON \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\"\n        AS PERMISSIVE FOR SELECT TO ${roleList} USING (true);\n    END\n    $owner_read$`);\n  logger.log(`Owner read policy ensured on \"${schema}\".\"${tableName}\" for ${owners.join(', ')}`);\n}\n\nasync function discoverTimescaleTableState(\n  qr: QueryRunner,\n  schema: string,\n  logger: RlsHelperLogger,\n): Promise<TimescaleTableState> {\n  assertSafeIdentifier(schema, 'schema');\n\n  const metadataColumns = await queryRows<{ column_name: string }>(\n    qr,\n    `\n      SELECT column_name\n      FROM information_schema.columns\n      WHERE table_schema = 'timescaledb_information'\n        AND table_name = 'hypertables'\n        AND column_name = ANY($1::text[])\n    `,\n    [['columnstore_enabled', 'compression_enabled']],\n  );\n\n  const supportedColumns = new Set(metadataColumns.map((row) => row.column_name));","numLines":70,"startLine":420,"totalLines":761}}}

The table does carry a `tenant_id` column (automation-program.entity.ty:139 / automation-program.entity.ts:139), but that column is the RLS discriminator layered on top of physical per-schema separation, not evidence of one shared physical table holding many tenants' rows.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:342\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:326\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:413\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts:132\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/__tests__/tenant-scheduled-jobs.rls.postgres.spec.ts:149\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/entities/automation-program.entity.ts:139\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"premise:2\",\n      \"verdict\": \"contradicted\",\n      \"note\": \"The premise requires that neither the predicate, the helper, row-level security, nor the caller restricts the call to the current tenant. Two of those four do restrict it. (a) Row-level security: sensor-service registers RlsModule.forPoolService (app.module.ts:418), whose RlsConnectionBootstrap injects the `app.current_tenant` GUC on every pool checkout (rls.module.ts:53); the canonical policy is created `FOR ALL ... USING <clause> WITH CHECK <clause>` (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674) where the clause is `\\\"tenant_id\\\" = NULLIF(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid` (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:295), under FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY so the owning application role is also subject (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:65); `syncTenantSchemas` reinstalls that policy in every `tenant_<uuid>` schema (rls.module.ts:69). An UPDATE on this connection therefore cannot match a row whose tenant_id differs from the bound GUC. (b) The caller: prepareProgramBundleArtifact resolves the row first via findByIdOrFail(programId, tenantId) at automation.service.ts:1507, which runs `where: { id, tenantId }` (automation.service.ts:266) inside runInTenantTransaction (automation.service.ts:137), so `program.id` at line 1560 is a uuid primary key (automation-program.entity.ts:134) already proven to belong to the passed tenant; the sibling update in the same method additionally carries `tenant_id = :tenantId` (automation.service.ts:1524). The call's own predicate and `this.programRepo` do not scope the write — that part of the premise holds — but the premise is a conjunction over all four guards and fails on RLS and on the caller.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:418\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/rls.module.ts:53\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/rls.module.ts:69\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:295\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:65\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1507\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:266\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:137\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1524\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/entities/automation-program.entity.ts:134\"\n      ]\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"defect\",\n      \"verdict\": \"contradicted\",\n      \"note\": \"The defect claim is that this repository call can read or change another tenant's rows. No product change at automation.service.ts:1560 is required to resolve that claim, because the claim does not hold at this location. Trace the two possible runtime states of the ambient pool connection the call uses. With a tenant context present, checkout sets search_path to that tenant's schema (tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts:132) and binds `app.current_tenant` to the same tenant (rls.module.ts:53), so the FORCE-RLS policy with WITH CHECK (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674, apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:65) confines the UPDATE to that tenant's rows. With no tenant context, the GUC is cleared and the policy predicate matches nothing, so the statement affects zero rows — the fail-closed behaviour the method's own helper docblock describes (automation.service.ts:137). In both states the key is a generated uuid primary key (automation-program.entity.ts:134) that the pinned, tenant-filtered read at automation.service.ts:1507 already resolved inside the passed tenant's schema. A separate and real hazard does exist at this line and warrants a root-cause fix: the write runs on the ambient connection instead of inside the `withTenantSchema` boundary its own reads use, so when the ambient tenant context is absent or differs from the `tenantId` argument the status transition to DEPLOYING silently updates zero rows while the bundle path at scada-package.service.ts:1437 records the program as staged. The root-cause change is to move this write inside `withTenantSchema(tenantId, ...)` and take the repository from `tenantManagerRepo(manager, AutomationProgram, tenantId)`, matching automation.service.ts:153 and automation.service.ts:2658, which also makes the tenant predicate structural. \"tool_id\": \"tenant-scoping-adapter\",\n      \"run_id\": null,\n      \"finding_id\": \"tenant-repository-unscoped-read:apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1560\",\n      \"verdict\": \"false_positive\",\n      \"judge_id\": \"aria-evidence-judge\",\n      \"model\": \"claude-opus-5\",\n      \"prompt_hash\": null,\n      \"confidence\": 0.86,\n      \"rationale\": \"Line 1560 is `await this.programRepo.update(program.id, { status: ProgramStatus.DEPLOYING, ... })` inside prepareProgramBundleArtifact. It names no tenant predicate and uses the plain injected repository, which is what the detector saw. Judging the product rather than the predicate, the claim that this call can read or change another tenant's rows does not hold, for three independent reasons in product code. First, `automation_programs` is a per-tenant-schema table: it sits in the per-tenant clone list at libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:342, the same file's comment at line 326 marks `vfd_command_audit_logs` as the deliberately cross-tenant ledger kept OUT of that list, apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:413 states sensor data lives in per-tenant schemas, and libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts:132 shows search_path being set to the tenant schema on every pool checkout. Second, row-level security binds the write: RlsModule.forPoolService (app.module.ts:418) installs the `app.current_tenant` GUC on each checkout (rls.module.ts:53), the canonical policy is FOR ALL with USING and WITH CHECK on the tenant column (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674, predicate at :295) under FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY so the owning role is also subject (:65), and it is reinstalled in every tenant_<uuid> schema (rls.module.ts:69). Third, the caller already proved ownership: automation.service.ts:1507 resolves the row through findByIdOrFail -> findById, whose query is `where: { id, tenantId }` inside runInTenantTransaction (automation.service.ts:266, :137), and `program.id` is a generated uuid primary key (automation-program.entity.ts:134), so the key cannot select a different tenant's row in the routed schema. There is a real, different defect at this line that should be fixed at the root: the write executes on the ambient pooled connection instead of inside the `withTenantSchema(tenantId, ...)` boundary the method's own reads use, so when the ambient tenant context is missing or differs from the `tenantId` argument the UPDATE matches zero rows under FORCE RLS and the DEPLOYING transition silently vanishes while scada-package.service.ts:1437 proceeds to publish the bundle. The root-cause change is to perform this update inside withTenantSchema and obtain the repository from tenantManagerRepo(manager, AutomationProgram, tenantId) (libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-scoped-repository.ts:545), as the sibling methods at automation.service.ts:153 and :2658 already do, which makes the tenant predicate structural instead of incidental. That is a write-atomicity and silent-no-op claim, not the cross-tenant exposure this finding states, so this finding's premises and defect claim are contradicted. Residual uncertainty, which is why confidence is 0.86 and not higher: policy installation in a live tenant schema has two code paths (the runtime sweep when db-migrate is not authoritative, app.module.ts:420, and the db-migrate fan-out), and no execution artifact in this request proves which ran for a given deployment; the per-tenant schema routing and the caller-side ownership proof hold independently of that, so the conclusion does not rest on RLS alone.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1560\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1507\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:266\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:137\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:153\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:2658\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/entities/automation-program.entity.ts:134\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/entities/automation-program.entity.ts:139\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:413\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:418\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:342\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:326\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts:132\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/rls.module.ts:53\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:295\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:65\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-scoped-repository.ts:545\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/process/services/scada-package.service.ts:1437\"\n      ],\n      \"judgment_group_id\": null,\n      \"finding_fingerprint\": null\n    },\n    \"excerpt_status\": \"The pinned excerpt covered automation.service.ts lines 1-233 while the finding is at line 1560, so the excerpt was insufficient for the cited call; the file was read at the snapshot and the excerpt's content for lines 1-233 matched what was read (import list, constructor at lines 95-123, withTenantSchema docblock and body at lines 128-138).\",\n    \"explanation\": \"Teaching the chain, concisely. The task: decide whether a product defect exists at automation.service.ts:1560, where a TypeORM update is keyed only by a primary key inside a tenant-aware method. Why it matters: in a multi-tenant platform a write that is not bounded to one tenant is the highest-cost class of bug, so the rule flags every such shape and a judge must then ask what the product actually does at that line. What the judgment turns on: this platform isolates tenants twice over, physically (each tenant has its own schema and the pool sets search_path at checkout) and logically (a FORCE-RLS policy keyed on the app.current_tenant GUC, applied FOR ALL with WITH CHECK). A primary-key-only update on such a table reaches exactly the rows of the tenant the connection is bound to, and the key itself came from a read already filtered by tenant inside a pinned transaction. What breaks if this reasoning is skipped: marking the finding true_positive would send an implementer to add a redundant predicate while the actual hazard at the same line stays open, and the hazard is different in kind. The ambient connection is not the pinned tenant transaction the method's reads use, so a caller without request-scoped tenant context makes this status write affect zero rows; the bundle then publishes while the program row never leaves APPROVED. Downstream surface affected: the release-bundle deploy path in scada-package.service.ts, the deployment-log correlation it hands to the edge, and any status-driven UI or reconciliation that trusts DEPLOYING. What proves the result: the per-tenant clone list and the contrasting cross-tenant ledger comment in schema-manager.service.ts, the search_path injection in tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts, the policy DDL in apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts, and the tenant-filtered read plus uuid primary key in automation.service.ts and the entity file — each cited at a line above.\"\n  }\n}\n```"}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2692,"cache_read_input_tokens":91420,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2692},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0758df33-8c42-48c2-9267-90bb24a8128c","uuid":"823bba5b-cf5f-4cb6-9161-60a4edf7366c","timestamp":"2026-10-08T09:45:23.580Z","request_id":"req_011CfpZ1HtXzKouGA7eWUBvo"}

USING <clause> WITH CHECK <clause>` (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674) where the clause is `\\\"tenant_id\\\" = NULLIF(current_setting('app.current_tenant', true), '')::uuid` (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:295), under FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY so the owning application role is also subject (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:65); `syncTenantSchemas` reinstalls that policy in every `tenant_<uuid>` schema (rls.module.ts:69). Trace the two possible runtime states of the ambient pool connection the call uses. With a tenant context present, checkout sets search_path to that tenant's schema (tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts:132) and binds `app.current_tenant` to the same tenant (rls.module.ts:53), so the FORCE-RLS policy with WITH CHECK (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674, apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:65) confines the UPDATE to that tenant's rows. \"tool_id\": \"tenant-scoping-adapter\",\n      \"run_id\": null,\n      \"finding_id\": \"tenant-repository-unscoped-read:apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1560\",\n      \"verdict\": \"false_positive\",\n      \"judge_id\": \"aria-evidence-judge\",\n      \"model\": \"claude-opus-5\",\n      \"prompt_hash\": null,\n      \"confidence\": 0.86,\n      \"rationale\": \"Line 1560 is `await this.programRepo.update(program.id, { status: ProgramStatus.DEPLOYING, ... })` inside prepareProgramBundleArtifact. It names no tenant predicate and uses the plain injected repository, which is what the detector saw. Judging the product rather than the predicate, the claim that this call can read or change another tenant's rows does not hold, for three independent reasons in product code. First, `automation_programs` is a per-tenant-schema table: it sits in the per-tenant clone list at libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:342, the same file's comment at line 326 marks `vfd_command_audit_logs` as the deliberately cross-tenant ledger kept OUT of that list, apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:413 states sensor data lives in per-tenant schemas, and libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts:132 shows search_path being set to the tenant schema on every pool checkout. Second, row-level security binds the write: RlsModule.forPoolService (app.module.ts:418) installs the `app.current_tenant` GUC on each checkout (rls.module.ts:53), the canonical policy is FOR ALL with USING and WITH CHECK on the tenant column (apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674, predicate at :295) under FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY so the owning role is also subject (:65), and it is reinstalled in every tenant_<uuid> schema (rls.module.ts:69). Third, the caller already proved ownership: automation.service.ts:1507 resolves the row through findByIdOrFail -> findById, whose query is `where: { id, tenantId }` inside runInTenantTransaction (automation.service.ts:266, :137), and `program.id` is a generated uuid primary key (automation-program.entity.ts:134), so the key cannot select a different tenant's row in the routed schema. There is a real, different defect at this line that should be fixed at the root: the write executes on the ambient pooled connection instead of inside the `withTenantSchema(tenantId, ...)` boundary the method's own reads use, so when the ambient tenant context is missing or differs from the `tenantId` argument the UPDATE matches zero rows under FORCE RLS and the DEPLOYING transition silently vanishes while scada-package.service.ts:1437 proceeds to publish the bundle. The root-cause change is to perform this update inside withTenantSchema and obtain the repository from tenantManagerRepo(manager, AutomationProgram, tenantId) (libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-scoped-repository.ts:545), as the sibling methods at automation.service.ts:153 and :2658 already do, which makes the tenant predicate structural instead of incidental. That is a write-atomicity and silent-no-op claim, not the cross-tenant exposure this finding states, so this finding's premises and defect claim are contradicted. Residual uncertainty, which is why confidence is 0.86 and not higher: policy installation in a live tenant schema has two code paths (the runtime sweep when db-migrate is not authoritative, app.module.ts:420, and the db-migrate fan-out), and no execution artifact in this request proves which ran for a given deployment; the per-tenant schema routing and the caller-side ownership proof hold independently of that, so the conclusion does not rest on RLS alone.\",\n      \"evidence_refs\": [\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1560\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:1507\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:266\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:137\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:153\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/automation.service.ts:2658\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/entities/automation-program.entity.ts:134\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/automation/entities/automation-program.entity.ts:139\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:413\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/app.module.ts:418\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:342\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service.ts:326\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts:132\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/rls.module.ts:53\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:674\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:295\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/rls/apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts:65\",\n        \"libs/backend-common/src/database/tenant-scoped-repository.ts:545\",\n        \"apps/sensor-service/src/process/services/scada-package.service.ts:1437\"\n      ],\n      \"judgment_group_id\": null,\n      \"finding_fingerprint\": null\n    },\n    \"excerpt_status\": \"The pinned excerpt covered automation.service.ts lines 1-233 while the finding is at line 1560, so the excerpt was insufficient for the cited call; the file was read at the snapshot and the excerpt's content for lines 1-233 matched what was read (import list, constructor at lines 95-123, withTenantSchema docblock and body at lines 128-138).\",\n    \"explanation\": \"Teaching the chain, concisely. The task: decide whether a product defect exists at automation.service.ts:1560, where a TypeORM update is keyed only by a primary key inside a tenant-aware method. Why it matters: in a multi-tenant platform a write that is not bounded to one tenant is the highest-cost class of bug, so the rule flags every such shape and a judge must then ask what the product actually does at that line. What the judgment turns on: this platform isolates tenants twice over, physically (each tenant has its own schema and the pool sets search_path at checkout) and logically (a FORCE-RLS policy keyed on the app.current_tenant GUC, applied FOR ALL with WITH CHECK). A primary-key-only update on such a table reaches exactly the rows of the tenant the connection is bound to, and the key itself came from a read already filtered by tenant inside a pinned transaction. What breaks if this reasoning is skipped: marking the finding true_positive would send an implementer to add a redundant predicate while the actual hazard at the same line stays open, and the hazard is different in kind. The ambient connection is not the pinned tenant transaction the method's reads use, so a caller without request-scoped tenant context makes this status write affect zero rows; the bundle then publishes while the program row never leaves APPROVED. Downstream surface affected: the release-bundle deploy path in scada-package.service.ts, the deployment-log correlation it hands to the edge, and any status-driven UI or reconciliation that trusts DEPLOYING. What proves the result: the per-tenant clone list and the contrasting cross-tenant ledger comment in schema-manager.service.ts, the search_path injection in tenant-connection-bootstrap.service.ts, the policy DDL in apply-tenant-rls.helper.ts, and the tenant-filtered read plus uuid primary key in automation.service.ts and the entity file — each cited at a line above.\"\n  }\n}\n```","ttft_ms":5230,"type":"result","duration_ms":347460,"uuid":"4b74fb16-f7c5-48d1-b1d8-c7a8b411e963","ttft_stream_ms":1449,"time_to_request_ms":154,"first_content_frame_ms":1450,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
